## 2. Architecture
The app calls only `server/`. The server talks to:
- **vcmem.com hub** (WordPress, Vibes AI Assistant plugin in hub mode): accounts, e-mail OTP, memberships, AI chat (n8n workflow "Vibes Web Agent"), staff console, admin roles. REST namespace `vibes-ai/v1`.
- **vibesholding.com Projects Bank** (plugin `projects-directory-pro`): projects feed and unlocks.
- **Paymob KSA**: real-world services only, Intention API, per-payment notification URL pointing to the server.
- **App stores via RevenueCat** (later): membership purchase â server â hub activation.
- **News sources + OpenAI API** (later): classification, deduplication and ranking only.

### 2.1 Hub bridge (later milestone)
The hub needs server-to-server endpoints secured by a bridge key. They are added to the Vibes AI Assistant plugin in a Claude chat session where the owner uploads the current plugin zip. Never guess the plugin's code.
Needed: register with OTP, verify/resend OTP, login returning a token for the server, profile get/update/avatar, delete account, membership status (unactivated, or annual with days left, AI Â«Ø±ØµÙØ¯Â», project unlocks left), activate membership from a store purchase, chat send (text/voice/image) and history, staff-reply webhook to the server (for push), admin check.

### 2.2 Projects Bank feed
- `GET https://vibesholding.com/wp-json/pb/v1/projects?key=â¦` (plus `/ping`). The key lives in server env only.
- Post type `project`. Meta: project_number, company_name, founder_name, whatsapp, email, website, pitch_deck, project_gallery, project_details (Arabic body; post_content is empty), project_details_en, excerpt_en, title_en, company_name_en, founder_name_en, is_featured (golden flag), featured_order, golden_partner_url, views_count. Taxonomies: `sector`, `project_stage`.
- whatsapp, email, website and pitch_deck are private (CLAUDE.md rule 4).
- Unlocks live in table `wp_pdp_unlocks`, keyed by the hub contact id. A paid member gets 5 project unlocks per membership year. Golden projects never deduct credit and route to golden_partner_url.
- A server-to-server unlock endpoint is added to the Projects Bank plugin in a later milestone (the owner uploads the plugin zip in a Claude chat).
- Sort options used on the web: Ø§ÙØ£Ø­Ø¯Ø« / Ø§ÙØ£ÙØ«Ø± ÙØ´Ø§ÙØ¯Ø© / Ø§ÙØªØ´Ù / Ø§ÙÙØ´Ø§Ø±ÙØ¹ Ø§ÙØ°ÙØ¨ÙØ©.

  - Status (2026-09-20, branch `m9-admin-posts`, commits 24a8f5b server, 1143040 dashboard, 62f2d70 app; NOT pushed, NOT deployed, no bucket yet, so the box stays open). Server `server/src/media/`: `POST /api/admin/uploads` (admin) answers an upload ticket: with a bucket a presigned POST whose policy pins key, content type and size (the browser sends the file straight to the bucket), without one a signed `PUT /api/admin/uploads/put?ticket=` to the local disk store `UPLOADS_DIR` (development and tests only, `durable: false`, refused in production); `GET /api/admin/uploads/config`; public `GET /media/<key>` = 302 to a one-hour presigned link (bucket) or the file with ranges (disk); keys are server-made `posts/YYYY/MM/<uuid>.<ext>`; images JPG/PNG/WebP/GIF up to `UPLOAD_MAX_IMAGE_MB` (10), video MP4/MOV/WebM up to `UPLOAD_MAX_VIDEO_MB` (200), QuickTime is stored and served as MP4 (Android's browser refuses the QuickTime label), SVG/HTML refused. Posts: input `videoFile { url, poster }`, output `video { url, poster } | null` (older posts have no key), `images` unchanged (uploaded images are `/media/` URLs next to pasted links); saving checks our media with HEAD (`invalid_media`), files a post stops using are removed unless another post uses them, an opt-in daily sweep (`UPLOADS_SWEEP=1` and Postgres) removes day-old abandoned uploads and never runs when no post references any file; the server writes the bucket CORS rules from `ADMIN_ORIGINS` at boot only on Railway (`/health` -> `uploads.cors`). Variables in `server/.env.example`: `S3_BUCKET`, `S3_ENDPOINT`, `S3_REGION`, `S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` (set them by reference to the Railway Bucket), `S3_FORCE_PATH_STYLE`, `UPLOADS_DIR`, `UPLOAD_MAX_*`, `UPLOADS_SWEEP`. New packages: `@aws-sdk/client-s3`, `@aws-sdk/s3-presigned-post`, `@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner`. Dashboard: Â«Ø¥Ø¶Ø§ÙØ© ØµÙØ±Â» / Â«Ø¥Ø¶Ø§ÙØ© ÙÙØ¯ÙÙÂ» pickers, progress with cancel, thumbnail grid with reorder and remove, poster frame captured in the browser, limits checked before sending, warning when files are not durable. Verified: `npm test` 85 pass (13 in `src/m15.test.ts`: tickets, disk store, ranges, traversal, posts with media, clean-up, sweep, a presigned bucket ticket built offline), server / admin / app typecheck, admin build, app lint; in the browser against a local server (mock hub, disk store): two images and a WebM clip uploaded from the editor at 375px, SVG refused in Arabic, poster captured, publish, the public feed carries `video`, re-edit right after save keeps the files, two batches keep their picked order. Constraints: rule 2, the identity stays the hub's (the code is a step on top of the hub account, never a separate admin registry); store only a hash of the code, limit attempts and resends, never log the code; the app's member sign-in is not affected.   - Status (2026-09-20, branch `m9-admin-posts`, NOT pushed, NOT deployed, `ADMIN_OTP` stays 0 until the mail delay is measured, so the box stays open). Server: `server/src/auth/adminOtp.ts` (challenge store: code hashed with the challenge token, 60 s life from `ADMIN_OTP_SECONDS`, single use, 5 wrong codes end the challenge, first mail + 3 resends with a 20 s gap, challenge lives 10 min; the 60 s start when the mail has left, and a resend whose mail fails changes nothing; per hub account, whatever the address: one live challenge, 6 challenges and 10 wrong codes an hour, then `otp_cooldown`; steps run one at a time per challenge and per account because the KV has no atomic counter); `AuthService.adminLogin / adminVerify / adminResend`; routes `POST /api/admin/auth/login | verify | resend` (per-address limits). The hub still checks the password and the admin role (rule 2), the role is read again at verify; the session record gets `adminVerifiedAt`, and `dashboardGuard` (posts, uploads, push, payments, membership purchases) asks for it while `ADMIN_OTP=1` and for a new code after `ADMIN_SESSION_HOURS` (12); a lapsed dashboard session is revoked on its first refused request. `/api/admin/hq/*` keeps `adminGuard`: the app's reception screens and member sign-in are untouched. The code is only in the mail body (never subject, log, response or storage). No SMTP on the live hub = the dashboard refuses to sign in (`otp_mail_off`, fail closed); way back in: set `ADMIN_OTP=0` on Railway. `/health` -> `hub.adminOtp`: `off | on | no_mail`. On the mock hub without SMTP the code is the mock's public test code (local runs). Dashboard: `Login.tsx` code step with a seconds countdown, Â«Ø¥Ø¹Ø§Ø¯Ø© Ø§ÙØ¥Ø±Ø³Ø§ÙÂ» and Â«Ø±Ø¬ÙØ¹Â»; it signs in through `/api/admin/auth/login` in both modes and falls back to `/api/auth/login` only when the server answers 404 `not_found` (a server from before M18), so the dashboard and the API can deploy in any order; after the hub's own Â«pendingÂ» e-mail code it drops that member session and signs in again through the dashboard route. Verified: `npm test` 101 pass (16 in `src/m18.test.ts`), server and admin typecheck, admin build, and in the browser against a local server (mock hub, `ADMIN_OTP=1`): code step, wrong code, used challenge, resend, sign-in, posts load. A multi-agent review (12 agents) confirmed 8 findings; fixed: no per-account limit, resend state changed before the mail left, the code clock started before the send, lapsed sessions never revoked, the Â«pendingÂ» path ending in a refused session, no fallback for an older server, the lock-out note in `.env.example`. Known leftovers: a post editor open across the 12 h cut-off loses its unsaved draft on save (wanted: ask for the code in place without unmounting the editor, `App.tsx` + `PostEditor.tsx`); the per-address limits trust `request.ip` with `trustProxy: true` (check how Railway sets `X-Forwarded-For` before relying on them; the per-account budget does not depend on it); `/health` says `on` when SMTP is configured, not when mail really arrives. NOT verified: a real mail through the hosting SMTP and its delay. To turn on: owner pushes; on Railway set `ADMIN_OTP=1` (variable writes are the owner's), check `/health` -> `hub.adminOtp: 'on'`, sign in once and time the mail; if it takes longer than a minute raise `ADMIN_OTP_SECONDS` (e.g. 180). An unverified hub e-mail still goes through the hub's own code first, then signs in again for the dashboard code. M25 (publisher role) must reuse `dashboardGuard`.

  **ROOT CAUSE FOUND + PATCHED 2026-10-23 (owner confirmed still LTR on his English-language iPhone):** `expo-localization`'s iOS module, with `supportsRTL` alone, RESETS `forceRTL` from the DEVICE locale on every cold start (`LocalizationModule.swift: forceRTL(isRTLPreferredForCurrentLocale())`), wiping the JS-persisted direction before React loads â so an Arabic app on an English iPhone always renders LTR (Android never touches the persisted flag, which is why it behaves; an English-device Android user would NOT have hit this). FIX: `patch-package` added (app devDependency + `postinstall`), patch `app/patches/expo-localization+57.0.2.patch` makes iOS keep the persisted forceRTL exactly like Android; `src/i18n/direction.ts` then converges with its existing one-restart guard. NATIVE change â needs **iOS build 3** (replace build 2 on the 1.0 version page before Add for Review; take the store screenshots from build 3 so they're RTL-correct).
